[-h] [-m {got,stack,auto}] [-n ATTEMPTS] [--libc LIBC] [--req-params REQ_PARAMS] [--strcmp-got STRCMP_GOT] [--system-off SYSTEM_OFF]\\n              [--no-fingerprint]\\n              url command\\nZyxel GS1900 Pre-Auth RCE\\npositional arguments:\\n  url                   target URL (http://ip or https://ip:port)\\n  command               command to execute\\noptions:\\n  -h, --help            show this help message and exit\\n  -m {got,stack,auto}, --mode {got,stack,auto}\\n                        got=deterministic, stack=ASLR brute-force, auto=fingerprint then decide\\n  -n ATTEMPTS, --attempts ATTEMPTS\\n                        max attempts for stack mode (default: 10000)\\n  --libc LIBC           libc base address override\\n  --req-params REQ_PARAMS\\n                        reqParameters address override\\n  --strcmp-got STRCMP_GOT\\n                        strcmp GOT address override\\n  --system-off SYSTEM_OFF\\n                        system() offset in libc override\\n  --no-fingerprint\\nExamples:\\n  exp.py http://192.168.1.1 'echo rce\u003e/tmp/pwned'\\n  exp.py https://192.168.1.1 '/bin/busybox telnetd -p 2323 -l /bin/sh'\\n  exp.py http://192.168.1.1 -m stack -n 5000 'id\u003e/tmp/id'\\nNotes:\\n  Command must not contain: \u0026 (0x26), = (0x3D), newline, null byte.\\n  GOT mode is deterministic (1 request). Its latest firmware was released on June 12, 2026, and release notes contain: “Fixed Pre-authentication buffer overflow vulnerability (CVE-2026-7273).”\"}]},{\"type\":\"orderedList\",\"attrs\":{\"start\":2,\"type\":null},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"listItem\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"paragraph\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"link\",\"attrs\":{\"href\":\"https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/cve-2026-7273\",\"target\":\"_self\",\"rel\":\"noopener noreferrer nofollow\",\"class\":\"tiptap-link\"}},{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}},{\"type\":\"underline\"}],\"text\":\"CVE-2026-7273 description\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\" does not match what the exploit does. It mentions a “stack-based buffer overflow vulnerability,” but the exploit achieves arbitrary memory write via a global buffer (\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"code\"}],\"text\":\".bss\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\" section), not stack.\"}]}]}]},{\"type\":\"paragraph\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\"So, we analyzed the patch to understand exactly what it changes. Here’s what we found.\"}]},{\"type\":\"paragraph\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\"In different places, the patch replaces calls to \"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"code\"}],\"text\":\"sprintf\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\" with \"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"code\"}],\"text\":\"snprintf\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\". Two of them are in \"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"code\"}],\"text\":\"main\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\":\"}]},{\"type\":\"image\",\"attrs\":{\"src\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1793683610/blog/pre-auth-rce-in-zyxel-gs1900-exploited-in-the-wild-as-a-zero-day-figure-2_qyfihi.png\",\"alt\":null,\"title\":null,\"width\":691,\"height\":167,\"caption\":\"Figure 2. 2.90(Axxx.1)C0 x 2.90(Axxx.2)C0 main() function comparison\",\"size\":\"large\",\"transparent\":false}},{\"type\":\"paragraph\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\"In the code shown in Figure 2, \"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"code\"}],\"text\":\"name\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\" is a 64-byte stack-allocated buffer. As the \"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"code\"}],\"text\":\"authID\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\" parameter is attacker-controlled, a specially-crafted request can indeed overflow the \"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"code\"}],\"text\":\"name\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\" buffer if the code uses \"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"code\"}],\"text\":\"sprintf\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\", but this has little to do with what the exploit targets.\"}]},{\"type\":\"paragraph\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\"Additionally, the patch adds the following checks to \"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"code\"}],\"text\":\"web_util_initParaPairs\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\":\"}]},{\"type\":\"codeBlock\",\"attrs\":{\"language\":\"c\"},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"text\":\"for ( pos = strchr(queries, '\u0026'); pos != nullptr; pos = strchr(pos + 1, '\u0026') )\\n{\\n  dest = \u0026reqParameters[1024 * counter];\\n  memcpy(dest, src, pos - src);\\n  dest[pos - src] = 0;\\n  v10 = strchr(dest, '=');\\n  v11 = v10;\\n  if ( v10 != nullptr )\\n  {\\n    if ( v10[1] == '\u0026' )\\n      dest[512] = 0;\\n    else\\n      urlDecode(dest + 512, v10 + 1);\\n    *v11 = 0;\\n  }\\n  src = pos + 1;\\n  if ( ++counter == 140 ) // CHECK A: return if counter == 140\\n    return 0;\\n}\\nif ( -queries \u003c strlen(queries) )\\n{\\n  v13 = \u0026reqParameters[1024 * counter];\\n  v12 = strlen(src);\\n  memcpy(v13, src, v12);\\n  v13[strlen(src)] = 0;\\n  v14 = strchr(v13, c: '=');\\n  v15 = v14;\\n  if ( v14 != nullptr )\\n  {\\n    v16 = v14 + 1;\\n    v17 = strlen(v14 + 1);\\n    memcpy(v13 + 512, v16, v17);\\n    *v15 = 0;\\n  }\\n  ++counter;\\n}\\nv18 = counter;\\nif ( counter \u003e= 141 ) // CHECK B: resets counter to 140 if counter \u003e 140\\n  v18 = 140;\\ncounter = v18;\\nreturn 0;\"}]},{\"type\":\"paragraph\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\"These checks do not help against the exploit either. By the time Check A happens, \"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"code\"}],\"text\":\"counter\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\" is already corrupted. Thus, the equality test fails and the code proceeds to the out-of-loop if block that processes the last parameter. The \"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"code\"}],\"text\":\"if\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\" block then copies the 141th parameter from the malicious payload to \"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"code\"}],\"text\":\"reqParameters[1024 * counter]\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\", so the latest firmware version released as a fix to CVE-2026-7273 is vulnerable to the same exploit, which works without changes.\"}]},{\"type\":\"paragraph\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\"We reported this to Zyxel and they said that the CVE-2026-7273 vulnerability was related to a \"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"code\"}],\"text\":\"reqParameters\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\" overflow due to a lack of counter validation, despite the vulnerability description on their website referring to a stack overflow.\"}]},{\"type\":\"paragraph\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\"Furthermore, they consider what we reported as a separate vulnerability because we also mentioned the lack of a parameter size check.\"}]},{\"type\":\"heading\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0,\"level\":3},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\"CVE-2026-107441 patch\"}]},{\"type\":\"paragraph\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\"On Oct. 22, 2026, just a few weeks after we contacted the vendor, they \"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"link\",\"attrs\":{\"href\":\"https://community.zyxel.com/en/discussion/34420/switch-gs1900-series-v2-90-patch-3-firmware-release\",\"target\":\"_self\",\"rel\":\"noopener noreferrer nofollow\",\"class\":\"tiptap-link\"}},{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}},{\"type\":\"underline\"}],\"text\":\"released new firmware\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\" versions for the GS1900 series that include a fix for this vulnerability. The parameter parsing loop in this new version is as follows:\"}]},{\"type\":\"codeBlock\",\"attrs\":{\"language\":\"c\"},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"text\":\"for ( pos = strchr(queries, '\u0026'); pos != nullptr; pos = strchr(pos + 1, '\u0026') )\\n{\\n  dest = \u0026reqParameters[1024 * counter];\\n  if ( counter \u003e= 140 ) // the global counter is checked **before** the copy\\n    return 0;\\n  // memcpy was replaced with a wrapper function that limits\\n  // how many bytes are copied to the destination (2nd parameter)\\n  web_util_paramCopy(\u0026reqParameters[1024 * counter], 512, src, pos - src);\\n  v11 = strchr(dest, '=');\\n  v12 = v11;\\n  if ( v11 != nullptr )\\n  {\\n    if ( v11[1] == '\u0026' )\\n      dest[512] = 0;\\n    else\\n      urlDecode(dest + 512, v11 + 1);\\n    *v12 = 0;\\n  }\\n  src = pos + 1;\\n  ++counter;\\n}\"}]},{\"type\":\"paragraph\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\"This effectively fixes the vulnerability as the copy is now limited to 511 bytes per field and the number of parameters is checked before the parameter is copied into the global buffer.\"}]},{\"type\":\"heading\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0,\"level\":3},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\"Conclusion\"}]},{\"type\":\"paragraph\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\"Things are moving fast in the cybersecurity space. LLMs give great power to attackers (we believe the exploit is LLM-generated), but also to defenders (we used LLMs to set up emulated environments running the impacted firmware versions). It is also worth mentioning that this exploit was tied to an existing vulnerability, CVE-2026-7273, and the United States Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Cybersecurity Agency (CISA) \"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"link\",\"attrs\":{\"href\":\"https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2026/09/21/cisa-adds-one-known-exploited-vulnerability-catalog\",\"target\":\"_self\",\"rel\":\"noopener noreferrer nofollow\",\"class\":\"tiptap-link\"}},{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}},{\"type\":\"underline\"}],\"text\":\"published a Known Exploited Vulnerabilities (KEV) alert\"},{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\" the same day recommending device owners to upgrade the firmware, but because the threat actor was exploiting a different vulnerability, and patch for CVE-2026-7273 remained ineffective, this had no effect on protecting devices from being exploited by this threat actor.\"}]},{\"type\":\"paragraph\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}}],\"text\":\"Cases such as this highlight the urgency for a more reliable way of checking vulnerabilities in software. Advisories and version numbers certainly help, but they cannot be blindly trusted. We truly believe the definitive way to assess whether software components contain vulnerabilities is by looking at its binary code. That’s why we created BTP and we keep improving it to address the needs of this quickly changing threat landscape.\"}]},{\"type\":\"heading\",\"attrs\":{\"textAlign\":\"left\",\"indent\":0,\"level\":3},\"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"marks\":[{\"type\":\"textStyle\",\"attrs\":{\"color\":\"\"}},{\"type\":\"bold\"}],\"text\":\"Related Reliability Engineer at Binarly and the company's first infrastructure-focused hire, owning the entire platform end-to-end.\n\nHe has architected, refined, and optimized the cloud infrastructure and backend systems that power the Binarly solution, including GitOps-managed Kubernetes clusters with ArgoCD, large-scale Argo Workflows pipelines, multi-tenant cluster consolidation, and platform deployments across bare-metal, OpenShift, Talos, EKS, and GKE. needed\",\"button\":null},\"video\":{\"__typename\":\"AboutVideo\",\"videoResource\":{\"__typename\":\"ResourcesVideo\",\"templateType\":\"video\",\"published\":true,\"publishDate\":\"2025-04-22T22:00:00.000Z\",\"title\":\"Binarly Transparency Platform Adds Exploit Maturity Scoring\",\"description\":\"Binarly's Alex Matrosov unveils Transparency Platform v3.0, featuring a real-time Exploitation Maturity Scoring (EMS) system prioritization\",\"ogType\":\"article\",\"image\":\"\",\"breadcrumbs\":null},\"url\":\"https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=znt2r5aZ41E\",\"_sys\":{\"filename\":\"binarly-transparency-platform-adds-exploit-maturity-scoring\",\"basename\":\"binarly-transparency-platform-adds-exploit-maturity-scoring.mdx\",\"hasReferences\":null,\"breadcrumbs\":[\"videos\",\"binarly-transparency-platform-adds-exploit-maturity-scoring\"],\"path\":\"content/resources/videos/binarly-transparency-platform-adds-exploit-maturity-scoring.mdx\",\"relativePath\":\"videos/binarly-transparency-platform-adds-exploit-maturity-scoring.mdx\",\"extension\":\".mdx\"},\"id\":\"content/resources/videos/binarly-transparency-platform-adds-exploit-maturity-scoring.mdx\"}},\"missionStatement\":{\"__typename\":\"AboutMissionStatement\",\"title\":\"Our Mission\",\"subtitle\":\"Most binary analysis and software supply chain solutions rely on outdated tools and open-source feeds of vulnerability data. They map file hashes and versions to known CVEs and vulnerabilities similar to legacy antivirus software from the 90s. The pure volume of alerts generated by these solutions creates fatigue and an unmanageable demand for today's product and security teams. Changes in software development practices and the growing popularity of AI-generated code assistants will only exacerbate existing challenges.\\n\\nTo address today's risks, we adopted a new approach to secure the software supply chain – this is why we created Binarly.\\n\\nVulnerabilities in firmware or the software supply chain can undermine every other security investment. Binarly's mission is to create a shift left, focusing on changing the way we detect and respond to the growing number of threats and vulnerabilities at every layer of the supply chain\"},\"teamIntro\":{\"__typename\":\"AboutTeamIntro\",\"title\":\"Meet the Binarly Team\",\"subtitle\":\"In 2021, we gathered top program analysts, firmware and software security experts and went back to core principles.\",\"description\":\"We have solved complex security problems and developed highly scalable products for some of the most used systems in the world, including NVIDIA, Intel, Cisco, Cylance and Google.\\n\\nTogether, we launched the industry's first binary risk intelligence platform in 2023. Combining modern static analysis techniques with the latest research, advancements in machine learning and our deep industry expertise, we're decoding the software supply chain puzzle.\",\"images\":[{\"__typename\":\"AboutTeamIntroImages\",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1780067853/260529%20Small%20swapouts/strip-0_yhomks.jpg\",\"alt\":\"Team\"},{\"__typename\":\"AboutTeamIntroImages\",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1780067857/260529%20Small%20swapouts/strip-1_cyqmor.jpg\",\"alt\":null},{\"__typename\":\"AboutTeamIntroImages\",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1780067861/260529%20Small%20swapouts/strip-2_zsvpeo.jpg\",\"alt\":null},{\"__typename\":\"AboutTeamIntroImages\",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1780067865/260529%20Small%20swapouts/strip-3_l7rlmg.jpg\",\"alt\":null},{\"__typename\":\"AboutTeamIntroImages\",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1780067868/260529%20Small%20swapouts/strip-4_bke2fo.jpg\",\"alt\":null},{\"__typename\":\"AboutTeamIntroImages\",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1780067872/260529%20Small%20swapouts/strip-5_agzndd.jpg\",\"alt\":null},{\"__typename\":\"AboutTeamIntroImages\",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1780067875/260529%20Small%20swapouts/strip-6_etzhgt.jpg\",\"alt\":null}]},\"quote\":{\"__typename\":\"AboutQuote\",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1780067845/260529%20Small%20swapouts/pr-alex-m_qpcbrq.jpg\",\"quote\":\"\\\"From day one Binarly has been focused on solving the most difficult security challenges in the software supply chain. The reality is that the existing approaches haven't kept pace with the scale of these problems.\\\"\",\"name\":\"Alex Matrosov\",\"role\":\"Co-Founder\"},\"heads\":{\"__typename\":\"AboutHeads\",\"title\":\"The Founding Team Behind Binarly\",\"subtitle\":\"Our technologies are driven by deep technical security research expertise.\",\"teamMember\":[{\"__typename\":\"AboutHeadsTeamMember\",\"name\":\"Alex Matrosov\",\"slug\":\"alex-matrosov\",\"role\":\"Founder\",\"isResearcher\":null,\"description\":\"Alex is a leading expert in firmware security, reverse engineering, and advanced malware analysis. Formerly Chief Offensive Security Researcher at NVIDIA, Alex has authored the acclaimed book Rootkits and Bootkits and regularly speaks at top conferences like REcon, Black Hat, and DEF CON.\",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1790947945/alex-matrosov_wov1vm.jpg\",\"socials\":[{\"__typename\":\"AboutHeadsTeamMemberSocials\",\"platform\":\"github\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/matrosov\"},{\"__typename\":\"AboutHeadsTeamMemberSocials\",\"platform\":\"linkedin\",\"url\":\"https://www.linkedin.com/in/matrosov/\"},{\"__typename\":\"AboutHeadsTeamMemberSocials\",\"platform\":\"x\",\"url\":\"https://x.com/matrosov\"}]},{\"__typename\":\"AboutHeadsTeamMember\",\"name\":\"Sam Thomas\",\"slug\":\"sam-thomas\",\"role\":\"Chief Scientist\",\"isResearcher\":null,\"description\":\"Sam leads the development of methods for automated vulnerability discovery and large-scale binary analysis. With a decade in academia and experience building the Fugue binary analysis framework, Sam bridges academic innovation and real-world applications.\",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1790947957/sam-thomas_vpmidm.jpg\",\"socials\":[{\"__typename\":\"AboutHeadsTeamMemberSocials\",\"platform\":\"linkedin\",\"url\":\"https://www.linkedin.com/in/xorpse/\"},{\"__typename\":\"AboutHeadsTeamMemberSocials\",\"platform\":\"x\",\"url\":\"https://x.com/xorpse?s=20\"}]},{\"__typename\":\"AboutHeadsTeamMember\",\"name\":\"Yegor Vasilenko\",\"slug\":\"yegor-vasilenko\",\"role\":\"Sr. Principal Security Researcher - Head of Research\",\"isResearcher\":true,\"description\":\"Yegor Vasilenko is a security researcher with a strong background in reverse engineering and vulnerability assessment. Prior to joining Binarly, he spent a significant part of his career in malware analysis, incident response and static analysis automation. They translate their expertise into the detection and insight that the platform provides.\",\"teamMember\":[{\"__typename\":\"AboutDepartmentsDepartmentsTeamMember\",\"name\":\"Anton Ivanov\",\"slug\":\"anton-ivanov\",\"role\":\"Principal Security Researcher\",\"isResearcher\":true,\"description\":\"Anton brings deep experience in cybersecurity, spanning malware analysis, application security, and vulnerability research. At Binarly, he leads research of POSIX-based firmware, with a particular focus on Baseboard Management Controllers (BMCs), and contributes to the development of product features based on his research findings. He also designs the automated systems used to collect and curate datasets that power Binarly's research initiatives. Outside of work, Anton is an active bug hunter in the security community.\",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1789570694/anton-ivanov_epxpax.jpg\",\"socials\":[{\"__typename\":\"AboutDepartmentsDepartmentsTeamMemberSocials\",\"platform\":\"linkedin\",\"url\":\"https://www.linkedin.com/in/antav7/\"},{\"__typename\":\"AboutDepartmentsDepartmentsTeamMemberSocials\",\"platform\":\"x\",\"url\":\"https://x.com/ant_av7\"}]},{\"__typename\":\"AboutDepartmentsDepartmentsTeamMember\",\"name\":\"Chris McMahon Stone\",\"slug\":\"chris-mcmahon-stone\",\"role\":\"Principal Research Engineer\",\"isResearcher\":true,\"description\":\"Chris is a principal research engineer in our Program Analysis team. His current research focuses on the intersection of AI and binary analysis, particularly the use of LLMs for code similarity and Software Composition Analysis (SCA). \",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1788521430/chris-mcmahon-stone_gz0qqo.jpg\",\"socials\":[{\"__typename\":\"AboutDepartmentsDepartmentsTeamMemberSocials\",\"platform\":\"linkedin\",\"url\":\"https://www.linkedin.com/in/chris-mcmahon-stone-802b9899/\"}]},{\"__typename\":\"AboutDepartmentsDepartmentsTeamMember\",\"name\":\"Fabio Pagani\",\"slug\":\"fabio-pagani\",\"role\":\"Vulnerability Research Lead\",\"isResearcher\":true,\"description\":\"Fabio Pagani is a Vulnerability Research Lead at Binarly, where he combines static and dynamic analysis techniques to uncover critical vulnerabilities in the UEFI ecosystem. His work has led to the discovery of high-impact findings including LogoFAIL and PKfail, affecting devices from virtually every major OEM. Fabio bridges industry and academia, serving on the program committee of WOOT.\\n\",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1789570713/fabio-pagani_vs6lbl.jpg\",\"socials\":[{\"__typename\":\"AboutDepartmentsDepartmentsTeamMemberSocials\",\"platform\":\"linkedin\",\"url\":\"https://www.linkedin.com/in/pagani-fabio/\"}]},{\"__typename\":\"AboutDepartmentsDepartmentsTeamMember\",\"name\":\"Fernando Mercês\",\"slug\":\"fernando-mercs\",\"role\":\"Principal Security Researcher\",\"isResearcher\":true,\"description\":\"Fernando is a security researcher that has spent more than 10 years analyzing malware and investigating cyberattacks and APTs. At Binarly, he works with vulnerability detection in ELF binaries as his primary focus, and broader ecosystem support on the side. In his free time, he helps the NPO that he founded with free educational programs in computing and security and enjoys developing open source tools.\",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1788859338/fernando-merces_jexqoz.jpg\",\"socials\":null},{\"__typename\":\"AboutDepartmentsDepartmentsTeamMember\",\"name\":\"Francesco Evangelista\",\"slug\":\"francesco-evangelista\",\"role\":\"Senior Research Scientist\",\"isResearcher\":true,\"description\":\"Francesco is a research scientist specializing in firmware and binary analysis. He began his career as a penetration tester and later focused on techniques for discovering and exploiting UEFI firmware vulnerabilities, particularly through fuzzing. At Binarly, he works on developing tools and methods to detect both known and unknown vulnerabilities in binaries.\",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1789570728/francesco-evangelista_jrgkf3.jpg\",\"socials\":[{\"__typename\":\"AboutDepartmentsDepartmentsTeamMemberSocials\",\"platform\":\"github\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/francesco-ev\"},{\"__typename\":\"AboutDepartmentsDepartmentsTeamMemberSocials\",\"platform\":\"x\",\"url\":\"https://x.com/francesco_ev\"}]},{\"__typename\":\"AboutDepartmentsDepartmentsTeamMember\",\"name\":\"Lucy Tretiakova\",\"slug\":\"lucy-tretiakova\",\"role\":\"Data Engineer Researcher\",\"isResearcher\":true,\"description\":\"Lucy is a data engineer with a passion for math. At Binarly, she developed the Risk Score system for prioritizing security findings and works on ETL pipelines to bridge research data from the research team to backend and frontend teams. She also contributes to the research team's QA efforts and test infrastructure.\",\"image\":\"https://res.cloudinary.com/dlnhqmgqi/image/upload/v1791581505/lucy-tretiakova_actp82.jpg\",\"socials\":null},{\"__typename\":\"AboutDepartmentsDepartmentsTeamMember\",\"name\":\"Takahiro Haruyama\",\"slug\":\"takahiro-haruyama\",\"role\":\"Principal Security Researcher\",\"isResearcher\":true,\"description\":\"Takahiro Haruyama is a principal security researcher at Binarly, where he focuses on threat intelligence research. His research spans generic UEFI bootkit detection, vulnerable driver discovery automation, defeating compiler-level obfuscations, Internet-wide malware C2 scanning, and firmware/disk/memory anti-forensics. He has presented at major industry conferences, including RE//verse, REcon, Virus Bulletin, HITB, DFRWS, SANS DFIR Summit, and Black Hat
